# **ChainTorrent MVP**

## Problem Statement

JavaScript dependency distribution runs through a single centralized registry that is an availability chokepoint, a mutable source of truth, and an intermediary between the people who publish packages and the people who consume them. ChainTorrent's MVP replaces that path with a content-addressed swarm, a canonical on-chain identity registry, and transferable access entitlements, so that the distribution and identity model is proven with monetization out of bounds.

**Adoption runs individual developer first, and everything else follows from that.** A developer at a terminal gains cross-project reuse of whatever their machine has already fetched, swarm retrieval on popular packages, and installs that survive a registry outage. Build platforms adopt next and for their own reasons: a service running the same few thousand popular installs continuously, on always-on machines with real bandwidth, holds the ideal cache as a by-product of its own economics, and seeding that cache costs almost nothing once it exists. That makes them natural superseeders rather than participants anyone has to recruit, and it is why no privileged bootstrap host is part of this plan. CI and enterprise adoption arrive as a consequence of that sequence rather than as targets to be won early.

The sequence matters to this workplan because it determines what the MVP measures. Latency is judged against interactive local installs rather than pipeline tolerance, since a developer notices a doubled install where a pipeline does not. Cache reuse is the headline adoption metric, because it is the benefit the adopting population actually experiences. Registry-outage survival remains true throughout and is simply not the lead, since it matters most to the population that adopts last.

The design is specified in `docs/research/cryptography.md` and bounded in `docs/research/MVP Scope.md`. Undetermined decisions are held in the To-Do list below.

The cryptographic construction is specified; its research record is `docs/research/cryptography-research-notebook.md`, and its statement is `docs/research/cryptography-critical-path.md`. A validation harness on both pairing curves produces the sizes, timings, and gas against the configured piece-group size and curve defaults, and nothing waits on it: every node reads the piece-group size from the deployment record and depends only on the code it calls.

## Objectives

Deliver an install path for JavaScript dependencies that survives registry outages, serves popular packages from a peer swarm, and exercises the identity and entitlement pipeline end to end.

Prove distribution and identity. Willingness to pay and seeder compensation are out of bounds and are held in the To-Do list; the transaction flow is proven at a nominal price on assets the project publishes, as a test instrument rather than a monetization step.

Every undetermined decision is carried in the To-Do list, which every workplan that stems from this one inherits.

## Expected Outcome

A developer installs dependencies and receives a working `node_modules` assembled from a local content-addressed store, a peer swarm, or the ingest source as fallback, with canonical identity resolved on-chain and an access entitlement minted to their wallet.

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/run/generate_consumer` **The run's consumer of the generate family's factory: `GenerateConsumer`, an `IGenerateConsumer<F>` generic over the delivery proof's forms, constructed from the typed mint and transfer contexts, the admitted mapping identities, and the admitted proof identity, which on receiving the concrete the factory admitted calls its `emit` with those values and returns the concrete's `EmitReturn` whole; names no concrete, calls no factory, and admits nothing; owns the consumer, its constructor params, and its return alias; adds the module to the `run` module**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the harness writes the generated directory through whichever generate concrete `create_generate` constructs from the configured selection, and the concrete's type carries the borrowed adapters' types, so the factory hands it to a consumer rather than returning it; the run therefore holds a consumer that makes the emission, so that `harness-crypto/run/delivery_proof_consumer` passes the consumer to `create_generate` and no run module names a generate concrete, reads a configured value, or admits a context or an identity (the dependency map's `harness-crypto/run/generate_consumer` row, its Dependencies edges, and its conflict flags on the harness run alone calling the factories and on the run being a composing function over consumers it owns; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope for `harness-crypto/run/generate_consumer`, calling `emit` with the typed contexts and the admitted identities; `harness-crypto/generate/factory`'s `construction`, which names this node as the emission, one `IGenerateConsumer<F>` generic over the forms in its own module)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the consumer is constructed once from a mint context typed `DeliveryContext<F, MintPurpose>`, a transfer context typed `DeliveryContext<F, TransferPurpose>`, the admitted identities the identity mapping covers, and the admitted identity the proofs are made for, each handed typed and admitted, and holds them for every emission it makes
    * `[✅]`   Functional: handed a concrete by the factory, `consume_generate` calls the concrete's `emit` once with `EmitParams` and an `EmitPayload` holding the mint context, the transfer context, the identities, and the proof identity it holds, and returns the `EmitReturn` the call returned, a success's files in write order and a refusal's error, whole and unaltered
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the consumer is received only with its own forms: it implements `IGenerateConsumer<F>` for the forms its contexts are typed in, so the factory hands it only a concrete whose `Forms` are `F`
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the consumer names no concrete, curve, kind, path, count, version, or file; reads no file, environment variable, or clock; calls no factory; rebuilds no form, admits no context or identity, and pairs no identity with a setup scope; every value it emits with arrives in its constructor and every behavior of the emission is the concrete's

  * `[✅]`   `role`
    * `[✅]`   App module of the harness in the adapter role, beneath its `run` module: a class implementing a repo-owned trait, the `IGenerateConsumer<F>` that `harness-crypto/generate/factory` declares, holding the values it emits with and wrapping no outer-edge collaborator of its own, as `harness-crypto/benchmark` is the pairing family's consumer
    * `[✅]`   Owns `GenerateConsumer`, `GenerateConsumerConstructorParams`, `GenerateConsumerTryNewReturn`, and `GenerateConsumerReturn`

  * `[ ]`   `objective`
    * `[ ]`   Problem: the harness composes the key agreement family over whichever KEM concrete `create_kem` constructs from the configured selection, and the concrete's type is the KEM type the key agreement consumer is generic over, so the factory hands it to a consumer rather than returning it; the run therefore holds a consumer that receives the concrete, builds the identities the later steps act on from the configuration's admitted identities, builds the next consumer in the chain over the concrete, and calls the key agreement family's factory, so that `harness-crypto/run/chain_forms_consumer` passes the consumer to `create_kem` and no run module names a KEM or key agreement concrete, reads a configured value, or admits a context or an identity (the dependency map's `harness-crypto/run/kem_consumer` row, which builds an `AdmittedKemIdentity` for the proof identity and each mapping identity by borrowing the configuration's admitted identities, each paired with its setup scope, then calls `create_key_agreement` with the configured key agreement selection, its consumer `harness-crypto/run/key_agreement_consumer`, and carries the typed contexts, the admitted identities, and the configured verifier concrete of the curve it composes, which carries that curve's deployment record, and the measurement settings to the consumer it constructs; its conflict flags on the harness run alone calling the factories and on the run being a composing function over consumers it owns; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope for `harness-crypto/run/kem_consumer`)
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the consumer is constructed once from the pairing, the hash-to-scalar adapter, the encoder, and the randomness source, each borrowed, a mint context typed `DeliveryContext<F, MintPurpose>`, a transfer context typed `DeliveryContext<F, TransferPurpose>`, the `AdmittedIdentities` the configuration admitted, borrowed, the `AdmittedKeyAgreement` selection, the `DeliveryProofConcrete` selection, the `GenerateConcrete` selection and its `Vec<VectorKindConfiguration>`, the `VerifierConcrete` of the one curve the consumer composes, the delivery-statement version as a `u16`, and the iteration count as a `NonZeroU32`, each handed typed and admitted, and holds them for every call it makes
    * `[✅]`   Functional: handed a KEM concrete by the factory, `consume_kem` builds one `AdmittedKemIdentity` for the held proof identity and one for each held mapping identity, in the order held, by pairing the admitted identity's variant with the setup scope and the `KemIdentity` that variant names, constructs one `KeyAgreementConsumer` from the held adapters, the received concrete, clones of the held contexts, those identities, and the held configured values, and calls `create_key_agreement` once, over the held pairing, hash-to-scalar adapter, encoder, and randomness source, with the held key agreement selection's `concrete`, `identifier`, and `algebra`; a refusal of `create_key_agreement` returns, and a refusal the key agreement consumer returns as its output returns; when both succeed it returns the key agreement consumer's success, whole and unaltered
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the consumer is received only with its own pairing: it implements `IKemConsumer<P>` for the pairing it borrows, so the factory hands it only a concrete whose `Pairing` is `P`
